Let's design high-performance local optimizer:

Internal objective function: given p (float64, sum=1), compute C=fft_convolve_self(p), return C.max() (or optionally objective). For sum not exactly 1, objective =2n*Cmax/(S^2). But we'll maintain sum=1 via normalization/projection.

Normalization: `normalize_simplex(p)`: p=np.maximum(p,0); S=p.sum(); if S>0 p/=S; else uniform. Clip to [0,1e6]? Values average ~1e-5, fine. If some p_i very large? Local optimization may produce but normalize.

Resample: cumulative linear interpolation.
```
def resample_sequence(p, m):
    p = np.asarray(p,float).clip(0,None)
    s=p.sum(); if s<=0 uniform.
    p=p/s
    F=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(p)))  # length n+1
    # Interpolate F at x=j/m for j=0..m, effectively F(x) for x in [0,1] linearly between nodes i/n.
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,m+1)
    y_new=np.interp(x_new, np.linspace(0,1,n+1), F)
    q=np.diff(y_new)
    # ensure sum=1
    return q/q.sum()
```
Since F monotone; interp may small negative due float; clip.

But if we resample to non-integer lengths, objective length changes. Use powers of two.

FFT convolution:
```
def conv_self(a):
    n=len(a); size=2*n-1; fsize=1<<(size-1).bit_length()
    fa=fft(a, fsize); c=np.real(ifft(fa*fa)); return c[:size]
```
For speed, when p sum=1, max C ~1e-5. To select active lags, need C. We can use same C.

Gradient for weights λ over selected lags:
- Input p, C, lags array (ints). We can choose active = np.where(C >= Cmax - tol)[0], but if many (plateau) too many? For polynomial-like density, active may be entire interval? Nonconvex maybe only few peaks. We can limit to top K, e.g., K=64 or 128. But for λ optimization, more lags helps. We can also include lags with C > Cmax - δ where δ = max(1e-7, 0.02*Cmax) maybe. Since Cmax~1e-5, 0.02*Cmax=2e-7; maybe many if plateau. Cap K=256.
- Weighting: softmax over selected C with beta. For sum=1 and C~1e-5, beta must be 1/(scale) e.g. 1e5. We can use beta = 1000 / (Cmax - C0?)? Or set weights proportional to C^γ? Since all near max, maybe uniform or exponential. I'd choose `temp = max(Cmax * 0.01, eps)`; weights = exp((C - Cmax)/temp). This gives weight 1 for max, 0.37 for 1% below. Focus but not too narrow. Or use top K uniform. We can experiment/variate.

Gradient via FFT: `grad_weights(p, lags, weights)`:
```
w = np.zeros(2*n-1); idx=lags; w[idx]=weights  # if weights sum=1. 
fsize = 1 << (len(w)+n-1).bit_length()
W=fft(w, fsize); A=fft(p, fsize)
g=2.0*real(ifft(W * conj(A)))[:n]
```
Check: If weights sum=1, g_i=2∑ λ p_{t-i}. Good. Could have tiny negative due FFT; clip? gradient can be negative? p, λ nonnegative => g nonnegative? Maybe use log-domain Adam; relative updates directly. Let's focus on robust log-domain.

Log-domain Gradient Descent (multiplicative) algorithm:
State x (log probabilities), p=softmax(x). But storing x permits unbounded. Objective C=sum over anti-diag p_i p_j. Gradient wrt x (for active weights): grad_x = 2 p_i ∑_t λ_t p_{t-i}. This is nonnegative. Descent: x -= lr * grad_x. Since adding constant to x no effect, we can normalize x by subtract mean each step or rely softmax. Multiplicative update p_i <- p_i exp(-lr grad_x_i). This can be done directly without x? Store p; update p_i *= (1 - η p_i g_p_i)? For small η, equivalent. But to allow larger steps, use exp. Since p finite. Algorithm:
```
p = normalize(init)
x = log(p + eps)
m=zero, v=zero, t=1
while time:
  C=conv(p); score=C.max(); update best
  active/weights
  # compute g_p = grad_weights(p, active, w)
  grad_x = p * g_p
  # maybe subtract mean? Since softmax invariance, subtracting mean of grad_x reduces variance and no effect on p? Actually x update with mean removal same p. Do grad_x -= grad_x.mean()
  # Adam step
  m = (1-b1)m + b1 grad_x
  v = (1-b2)v + b2 grad_x^2
  mh = m/(1-b1**t); vh = v/(1-b2**t)
  x -= lr * mh / (np.sqrt(vh)+eps)
  # To keep x stable, subtract mean(x)
  x -= x.mean()
  p = exp(x); p = p / p.sum(); clip tiny
```
But note grad_x is always nonnegative; after subtract mean, some coordinates negative (increase p) and some positive (decrease). This can raise low-interaction points, which may be needed to maintain sum. Descending in log p reduces high-interacting coordinates and raises low ones. Good.

Adam with lr maybe 0.01-0.1 in log scale. Each step adjusts p by ~lr. For log scale, 0.01 is 1%, okay. Could need thousands steps. FFT per iteration ~1ms, thousands = few seconds. We can do many.

However Adam on nonconvex max might not make monotonic progress; use line search / restarts. Let's design `linear_polish` robust with active weights and line search.

Function `linear_polish(p_in, n, max_time, rng, topk=64, mode='lin', restarts?)`
Pseudo:
```
def linear_polish(p_in, n, max_time, rng, topk=64, beta_temp=0.01, step_base=10.0):
    p=normalize_prob(p_in)
    y=p.copy(); t=1.0
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=objective_internal(p)
    start=time.monotonic(); it=0
    prev_score=best_score
    step_scale=step_base  # alpha relative to mean
    while time < max_time:
        it+=1
        c=conv_self(y)
        sc=c.max()
        if sc<best_score: update best (also maybe p if improvement)
        # select active topk
        lags, weights = select_weights(c, topk, temp scaling)
        g=grad_weights(y, lags, weights)
        g=np.maximum(g,0)
        gnorm=np.linalg.norm(g)
        if gnorm < eps: break
        g_u = g / gnorm
        mean_p = 1.0/n
        # FISTA momentum uses y; compute x_new from y? Actually FISTA: y is extrapolated, a is previous. We'll do simple projected gradient with y maybe.
        # step = step_scale * mean_p maybe. If gnorm after normalize 1.
        step = step_scale * mean_p
        q = project_simplex(y - step*g_u, 1.0)
        # line search along q
        best_try = None; best_try_score = sc + 1e-15
        alphas = [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625]
        # Also try with larger step? If no improvement at 1, try scaling step? Maybe if q far, small alpha should improve. If none, perhaps g direction bad? We can perturb.
        for a in alphas:
            cand = y + a*(q-y)  # since q from y
            cand = normalize_prob(cand)
            c2=conv(cand); s2=c2.max()
            if s2 < best_try_score:
                best_try_score=s2; best_try=cand; best_a=a
        if best_try is None:
            # no improvement; perturb y by noise or accept small step using previous step_scale decrease
            # Try multiplicative log update
            cand = multiplicative_update(y, g, eta=step_scale)
            s2=...
            if s2<sc: y=normalize_prob(cand); best_a=1; step_scale*=0.7
            else:
                y = y + 0.01*(q-y); step_scale*=0.5
        else:
            y=best_try
            # adapt step_scale: if best_a==1, can increase slightly; else decrease toward 1/best_a?
            if best_a >= 0.999:
                 step_scale = min(step_scale*1.05, 1000)
            else:
                 step_scale = max(step_scale*best_a*0.8, 0.1)
        # FISTA update? We used y as current; no momentum. Could add momentum:
        # x_new = y; y_next = x_new + ((t-1)/(t_new))*(x_new - a)
```
But if we use FISTA, y can be outside simplex? We project and clip. Need track a previous. Maybe simple gradient with line search is enough, but FISTA helps. We can implement FISTA with step normalized:
```
x = a.copy(); t=1
y initialized x
loop:
  compute g at y
  step = step_scale/n
  x_new=project_simplex(y - step*g_u)
  line search from y to x_new: choose alpha
  a = y + alpha*(x_new-y)
  t_new=...
  y = a + ((t-1)/t_new)*(a - x_prev)
  y=project_simplex(y,1)
```

Log-domain with active weights and backtracking line search:

- C=conv(p); score.
- active weights; g_p = grad_weights(p, lags, weights); grad_log = p * g_p. Since if p_i=0, grad=0; use epsilon.
- Center: Since x shift by constant has no effect, we can subtract mean of grad_log to keep x stable: `d = grad_log - grad_log.mean()`. But descent in x should be -grad_log. Subtracting mean doesn't change p update if we then normalize? If update x - step*d, p_new=exp(x-step*d)/sum, same as exp(x-step*grad_log) normalized. So we can just scale grad_log by its norm to avoid step huge. `g = grad_log / (norm2(grad_log)+eps)`, then d = g (no center needed, normalization handles overall shift but for numerical stability subtract mean): `d = g - g.mean()`. Then search α: p_try = softmax(x - α d) (or directly p * exp(-α d) normalize). Since d centered, exp values moderate. α step has no physical units (log change magnitude). Let's outline a `local_optimize(p, max_time, rng, n, qpsolve_interval=10, topk=48)`:
- State p (sum=1), best.

- For it in time:

  - Compute h = gradient_log weighted. For QP weights, compute H@w (or via FFT if no H). For fast iteration, use `grad_log_fast = p * grad_weights(p, lags, weights)` (FFT). For QP, compute directly from H maybe also store `base_h`? We can just FFT.
  - Center and normalize d = h - mean(h); if norm small break.
  - Line search α using multiplicative update: p_try = p * exp(-α d); normalize. Use α candidates around previous. Evaluate. Choose best improving. If none, try smaller α or apply tiny random perturbation.
  - Update p; adapt α.
- Also periodically try linear projected step or log with different weights.

Construct H for lags and compute h directly:
```
def build_log_grad_matrix(p, lags):
    n=len(p); K=len(lags); H=np.empty((n,K))
    for col,t in enumerate(lags):
        h=np.zeros(n)
        if t < n:
            if t>=0:
                h[0:t+1] = 2.0 * p[t::-1]  # p[t],...,p[0]
        else:
            start = t - (n - 1)
            end_j = n - 1
            beg_j = t - (n - 1)
            # j from end_j down to beg_j for i start..n-1
            h[start:n] = 2.0 * p[beg_j:end_j+1][::-1]
        H[:,col]=h
    return H
```
Check for t=0: h[0:1]=2p[0]; ok. t=n-1: h[0:n]=2p[n-1::-1]=2p reversed? derivative of central C: ∑ p_i p_{n-1-i}; derivative for i=0 is 2p_{n-1}, for i=n-1 is 2p_0, so h[0]=2p_{n-1}; correct.
For t=n: start=1, beg_j=1? t-(n-1)=1, end_j=n-1. h[1:n]=2*p[1:n][::-1]. Derivative at i=1 pair j=n-1, i=n-1 pair j=1? but t=n, pair i=0? j=n out of range, so i=0 none. Yes h[1]=2p_{n-1}. ok.
For t=2n-2: start=n-1, beg_j=n-1, h[n-1]=2p[n-1]. But central? For full conv last coefficient a_{n-1}^2 derivative 2a_{n-1}; yes.

But gradient_log = p * h. We can multiply H by p: `D = H * p[:,None]`. Then QP on D? If direction in log domain d_i=∑ w h_i = p_i∑ w derivative_i. The norm of centered d. So build D = H * p. Selecting active weights quickly:
```
def select_weights(c, K, mode):
    n=len(c)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:] if K < len(c) else np.arange(len(c))
    # sort descending
    idx = idx[np.argsort(c[idx])[::-1]]
    top_vals = c[idx]
    vmax = top_vals[0]
    if mode == 'uniform':
        w = np.ones(K)/K
    elif mode == 'softmax':
        temp = max(1e-300, vmax * 0.02) # 2% of max
        w = np.exp((top_vals - vmax)/temp); w/=w.sum()
    elif mode == 'power':
        # w = (top_vals/vmax)^10
        w = (top_vals/vmax)**10; w/=w.sum()
    return idx, w
```
Use mode cycle: maybe start with 'power' to focus steepest, then 'softmax', later 'uniform' for plateau. Actually to reduce global max, focusing on single highest peak effective; but may create new peaks. Cycles help.

QP weights:
```
def qp_weights(p, c, K=32):
    idx, w0 = select_weights(c,K,'power')
    n=len(p)
    D = build_log_grad_matrix(p, idx)  # D_ij = p_i * derivative_p for lag j = 2 p_i p_{t-i}
    Dc = D - D.mean(axis=0)
    A = Dc.T @ Dc
    # solve min w^T A w simplex
    w = w0.copy()
    trace = np.trace(A)/K
    if trace <= 0: return idx, w
    lr = 2.0/(trace + eps)
    for _ in range(20):
       grad = 2.0 * (A @ w)
       w = w - lr*grad
       w = project_simplex(w, 1.0)
    return idx, w
```

```
def linear_optimize(p_in, max_time, rng, topks=(1,4,16,32), eta0=1.0, use_momentum=True):
    p = normalize_prob(p_in)
    y = p.copy(); a = p.copy(); t=1.0
    eta = eta0
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=conv_self(p).max(); it=0; noimp=0
    while time:
       c=conv_self(y)
       if c.max()<best_score: update best
       if y differs from p? We can compute score at p too.
       K = cycle topks
       idx,w=select...
       g=grad_weights(y, idx, w); g=np.maximum(g,0)
       # If gnorm too small break.
       q = project_simplex(y - eta*g, 1)
       # line search from p? direction = q - p maybe if not momentum. If using p as reference:
       alphas = [1,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]
       best_cand=p; best_sc=score_p
       for alpha:
          cand = p + alpha*(q - p)
          cand = normalize_prob(cand)
          s2=conv_self(cand).max()
          if s2 < best_sc: best_sc=s2; best_cand=cand; best_alpha=alpha
       if best_cand not p:
          p=best_cand; noimp=0
          adapt eta: if best_alpha>=0.99: eta=min(eta*1.1, max eta 100); elif best_alpha<0.25: eta*=0.6
       else:
          noimp +=1
          if noimp>50: deep noise, p=best_p+noise, y=p, t=1, eta=1
       # momentum update y
       t_new = 0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4t*t))
       y = p + ((t-1)/t_new)*(p - a)
       y = project_simplex(y,1)
       a = p; t=t_new
       if noimp>... reset t=1
```

- y uses p and a.

Maybe use a simpler projection if most coordinates positive: To project onto simplex, we can find threshold by sorting. Implement fast:
```
def project_simplex(v, S=1.0):
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,n+1)) - (css - S) > 0)[0][-1]
    theta=(css[rho]-S)/(rho+1)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```
Uses sort. We can avoid S param by 1.

For q = project_simplex(y - ηg). If η small, threshold might be near min, sort heavy. ```

```
n=len(p)
# Build D only for K columns. To speed, use loops with slices, allocate D (n,K).
D = np.empty((n,K), dtype=np.float64)
for j,t in enumerate(idx):
   h = np.zeros(n) # but allocating each col heavy. Better fill D[:,j].
   if t < n:
      D[0:t+1, j] = 2.0 * p[t::-1]
   else:
      start = t - (n - 1)
      beg = t - (n - 1)
      D[start:n, j] = 2.0 * p[beg:n][::-1]
D *= p[:,None]  # log gradient
Dc = D - D.mean(axis=0)
A = Dc.T @ Dc
# Projected gradient on w simplex
w = w0.copy()
trace = np.trace(A)/K
lr = 2.0/(trace + 1e-30)
for _ in range(20):
   grad = 2.0*(A@w)
   w = w - lr*grad
   w = project_simplex(w, 1.0)
   w = np.maximum(w,0); w/=w.sum() # project already
# To ensure descent, mix with w0? maybe w = 0.7*w +0.3*w0? But QP may choose cancellation; mix safer.
w = 0.6*w + 0.4*w0; w/=w.sum()
return idx, w
```

Let's design `smooth_weight(c, beta_rel)`:
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta_rel):
    cmax = np.max(c)
    if cmax <= 0: w uniform?
    beta = beta_rel / cmax
    # To avoid underflow, w = exp(beta*(c-cmax)); underflow okay.
    s = w.sum()
```

```
def smooth_noise_direction(n, rng, num_terms=3):
    d=np.zeros(n)
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)

       phase = rng.uniform(0,2π)
       d += rng.normal(0,1)*np.sin(2π*freq*t+phase)
       d += rng.normal(0,1)*np.cos(...)
    d -= d.mean()
    return d / norm(d)
```

Detailed functions:

1. `fft_convolve_self(a)`:
```
def fft_convolve_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fsize = 1
    while fsize < size: fsize <<= 1
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))
    return c[:size]
```
Use `scipy.fft` imported as `from scipy.fft import fft, ifft`. For real, could use `np.fft` but scipy fine. `A*A` not conjugate (convolution, not autocross). Good.

2. `internal_objective(p)`:
```
def internal_objective(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.inf
    c = fft_convolve_self(p)
    cm = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    return 2.0 * len(p) * cm / (s*s)
```
But for normalized p sum=1, can use cmax only. We'll have `score_from_c(c,n) = 2*n*max(c)/(sum^2)`. Since we maintain sum=1, `score_obj = 2*n*cmax`. Compare via cmax. To avoid inconsistency due sum drift, in line search use p sum maybe exactly 1; if clip, renormalize. Use objective function for raw.

3. `normalize_prob(p)`:
```
def normalize_prob(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    s = p.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(len(p), dtype=np.float64)/len(p)
    return p/s
```
Maybe cap values? If p huge, normalization ok. For final, values <1000 automatically.

4. `project_simplex(v, S=1.0)`:
Use standard. Ensure output finite. If v contains nan/inf, fallback uniform.
```
def project_simplex(v, S=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx >= (css - S)  # maybe >0
    # Use last true
    if not np.any(cond):
        theta = 0.0? Actually if all false? For S positive, should some true.
    else:
        rho = int(np.max(np.nonzero(cond)[0])) + 1
        theta = (css[rho-1] - S)/rho
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    out_sum = out.sum()
    if out_sum <= 0: return np.ones(n)*S/n
    out *= S/out_sum
    return out
```
Need be careful cond formula: Project onto simplex sum S: `rho` satisfies `u[rho] - (css[rho]-S)/rho > 0`. In 1-index. Use `u * idx - (css - S) > 0`. For nonnegative S. If S=1, okay.
If all cond false (S very large?) fallback: `theta = np.mean(v)`? Actually if S > sum? We project sum S less? In our usage S=1 and v values around 1/n sum maybe close; should have. Robust: if no true, use uniform? But if S > sum(v)+n*max? Not. Use `theta = (np.sum(v)-S)/n`? Let's implement robust:
```
cond = u * idx - (css - S) > 0
if np.any(cond): rho=...
else: rho=n; theta=0? If S >= css[-1] maybe projection onto sum S would require adding, but simplex projection with S greater than sum? Not needed.
```
S=1, v sum maybe ~1, some cond.

5. `resample_sequence(p, m)`:
As above. Use `np.asarray, clip, sum`. For m maybe <2. Ensure nondecreasing F. Due float interp, diff can be tiny negative; clip.
```
def resample_sequence(p, m):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).clip(0,None); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return ones(m)/m
    p=p/s; n=len(p)
    if n==1: return ones(m)/m
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n+1)
    F=np.empty(n+1); F[0]=0; F[1:]=np.cumsum(p)
    # enforce F[-1]=1
    F = np.maximum.accumulate(F); F[-1]=1.0
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m+1)
    y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,F)
    q=np.diff(y)
    q=np.clip(q,0,None); s=q.sum(); if s<=0: uniform; else q/=s
    return q
```
For downsample, works.

6. `make_weights_array(c, mode, n, topk, beta_rel)`:
Take c clipped. Return w length len(c). Modes:
- 'full': full softmax.
- 'top': selected. Parameter `topk`, `submode`: 'power', 'soft', 'uniform'.
Could combine by encoding. To simplify, in optimizer use functions directly.
```
def weights_full_softmax(c, beta_rel):
    cnp = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(np.max(cnp))
    n = len(cnp)
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
       w=np.ones(n)/n; return w
    beta = beta_rel / cmax
    z = beta*(cnp - cmax)
    # Underflow for z<-745 to 0; okay. For very negative, exp 0.
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        w = np.zeros(n); w[int(np.argmax(cnp))]=1; return w
    w /= s
    return w

def weights_top(c, K, submode):
    cnp = np.asarray(c, dtype=float).clip(0,None)
    n=len(cnp)
    w=np.zeros(n)
    if K >= n:
        idx=np.arange(n)
    else:
        idx=np.argpartition(cnp, -K)[-K:]
    vals=cnp[idx]
    vmax=vals.max()
    if vmax <= 0:
       w[np.argmax(cnp)]=1; return w
    if submode == 'uniform':
       w[idx]=1.0
    elif submode == 'power':
       # if many zeros, (vals/vmax)**8
       w[idx]=(vals/vmax)**8.0
    elif submode == 'soft':
       temp=max(vmax*0.01, 1e-300)
       w[idx]=np.exp((vals-vmax)/temp)
    else: uniform
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w[idx[np.argmax(vals)]?]=1; else w/=s
    return w
```
For `soft`, if K=1, w[0]=1. For top sorted not needed.

7. `grad_array(p, w)`:
```
def grad_array(p, w):
    n=len(p); size=len(w)  # should 2n-1
    fsize=1; while fsize < len(w)+n-1: fsize <<=1? For linear cross-correlation zero-padded length at least size + n -1 = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2? Wait previous formula used fsize for len(w)+n-1. If w length 2n-1 and p length n, to compute linear cross-correlation (w[i+j]p[j]) for i=0..n-1, indices i+j up to n-1+2n-2=3n-3, length 3n-2. Need FFT length >=3n-2 to avoid circular aliasing. Did previous use `fsize = 1 << (len(w)+n-1).bit_length()` yes = size+n-1 =3n-2. For conv self, fsize 2n-1. For grad, use fsize>=3n-2. Good catch. For n=8192, fsize 24576? 3n=24576, fine. Use 1<<bit.
    W=fft(w, fsize); A=fft(p, fsize)
    g=np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A)))[:n]
    return 2.0*g  # if w sums to weights λ, derivative 2 w_conv * p
```
Let's verify formula: (ifft(W*conj(A)))_i = ∑_j W_{i+j} A_j (circular) if zero-padded with L >= len(W)+len(A)-1? We need W index i+j not modulo; length L. For i up to n-1, j up to n-1, i+j up to 3n-3, L>3n-2, no wrap. Good. Multiply 2. If w sum=1. `g_i=2∑_j w_{i+j}p_j`. w array indexed by t=i+j (0..2n-2). This matches derivative ∑_t λ_t 2p_{t-i} with j=t-i => t=i+j. Good.
Numerical negative small clip: `g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)` after real. But if w full softmax nonnegative, true g nonnegative. Good.

`grad_array` cost FFT length 3n-2; for n=32768 fsize=131072? 3n=98304, bit 131072. Good.

8. `score_from_conv(c, n, total_sum=1.0)`:

```
return 2.0*n*max(c)/(total_sum*total_sum)
```
But in optimizer p sum=1, just `2*n*cmax`. For comparing candidates, use `cmax` (since same n and sum). We'll use cmax to save.

9. `linear_optimize(p_in, max_time, rng, final_n?)`.
Let's write robust.

Pseudo implementation in detail:
```
def linear_optimize(p_in, max_time, rng, use_qp=False):
    p = normalize_prob(p_in)
    n = len(p)
    inv_n = 1.0 / float(n)
    c = fft_convolve_self(p)
    score = float(np.max(c))
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score = score; best_c = c.copy()
    eta = 0.05
    it = 0
    noimp = 0
    start = time.monotonic()
    # mode cycle list
    modes = [
       ('full', 10.0),
       ('top', 16, 'soft'),
       ('full', 40.0),
       ('top', 64, 'uniform'),
       ('full', 3.0),
       ('top', 4, 'power'),
       ('top', 32, 'soft'),
    ]
    last_qp = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it += 1
       # if p changed? c already current; if not, use current
       cmax = float(np.max(c))
       if cmax < best_score:
           best_score=cmax; best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy()
       # choose mode
       mode = modes[it % len(modes)]
       if mode[0]=='full':
           w = weights_full_softmax(c, mode[1])
       else:
           w = weights_top(c, mode[1], mode[2])
       # optional QP: if use_qp and it % 20 == 0:
       #    idx,_ = top lags? But weights_top returns full. We can call qp_weights.
       # For now skip QP.
       g = grad_array(p, w)
       g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
       gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
       if gn < 1e-300:
           # try noise
           ...
           continue
       g = g / gn
       # candidate steps
       cands = []
       base = eta * inv_n
       # factor 1
       q1 = project_simplex(p - base * g, 1.0)
       # candidate factor1 endpoint
       cands.append((q1, 1.0))
       # factor 0.5 by interpolation
       if base > 0:
           q_half = p + 0.5*(q1 - p)
           q_half = np.clip(q_half, 0, None)
           ssum=q_half.sum()
           if ssum>0: q_half /= ssum
           cands.append((q_half, 0.5))
           q_quarter = p + 0.25*(q1 - p); ...
       # factor2 if eta < 10:
       if eta < 10.0:
           q2 = project_simplex(p - 2.0*base*g, 1.0)
           cands.append((q2, 2.0))
       # evaluate
       best_cand = None; best_s = score + 1e-300; best_factor=0
       for cand, factor in cands:
           cc = fft_convolve_self(cand)
           cs = float(np.max(cc))
           # if cs significantly lower
           if cs < best_s:
               best_s=cs; best_cand=cand; best_cand_c=cc; best_factor=factor
       if best_cand is not None and best_s < score - 1e-14:
           p = best_cand; c = best_cand_c; score = best_s
           noimp = 0
           if best_factor >= 2.0:
               eta = min(10.0, eta*1.5)
           elif best_factor >= 1.0:
               eta = min(10.0, eta*1.1)
           elif best_factor < 0.5:
               eta = max(0.001, eta*0.7)
           else:
               eta = max(0.001, eta*0.9)
       else:
           noimp += 1
           if noimp == 1:
               eta = max(0.001, eta*0.7)
           if noimp % 20 == 0:
               # add smooth noise to best or current
               if noimp > 80 or (noimp > 30 and rng.random() < 0.3):
                   p = add_smooth_noise(best_p, rng, n, scale=0.1 + 0.3*(noimp/100.0))
                   c = fft_convolve_self(p); score = float(np.max(c))
                   if score < best_score: update
                   noimp = 0; eta=0.05
                   # also maybe replace with best if current worse?
       # limit time
```
Potential issue: When using `modes[it % len]`, if `top` with K close to full, building w_top uses argpartition O(n). Fine.

`add_smooth_noise`:
```
def add_smooth_noise(p, rng, n, scale=0.1):
    t = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    d = np.zeros(n)
    for k in [1,2,3,5,8]:
        freq = int(k * rng.integers(1,4))
        phase = rng.uniform(0, 2*np.pi)
        d += rng.normal(0,1)*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*t + phase)
    d -= d.mean()
    dn=np.linalg.norm(d)
```

```
def make_fourier_seed(n, rng, max_terms=5):
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    base = np.ones(n)
    for k in range(1,max_terms+1):
       f = rng.integers(1,6)  # low freq
       phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       amp = rng.uniform(-0.3,0.3) * (1.0/k)
       base += amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*t+phase)
    base = np.maximum(base, 1e-3)
    return normalize_prob(base)
```
Also exponential ramps variants.

```
def qp_weights_linear(p,c,K=24):
    idx, w_top = top weights array? Need top weights values. We can get idx and vals.
    H = np.empty((n,K))
    for j,t...
       H[...] = 2.0 * p[...]
    Hc = H - H.mean(axis=0)
    A = Hc.T @ Hc
    w = top_weights (normalized over K)
    lr=2/(trace(A)+eps)
    for 15:
       w = w - lr*2*(A@w)
       w = project_simplex(w,1.0)

    w_qp = w
    w = 0.5*w_qp + 0.5*w_top
    full = zeros; full[idx]=w; return full
```

Implement `project_simplex` robust with fast path.
```
def project_simplex(v, S=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size ==0: return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
       v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    n=v.size
    min_v = float(np.min(v))
    sum_v = float(np.sum(v))
    if min_v >= 0.0:
        theta = (sum_v - S) / float(n)
        if theta <= min_v:
            out = v - theta
            # due to roundoff, sum exactly S? adjust
            out_sum = float(np.sum(out))
            if out_sum <=0: return np.ones(n)*S/n
            return out * (S/out_sum)
    # fallback sort
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx - (css - S) > 0.0
    if np.any(cond):
       rho = int(np.max(np.nonzero(cond)[0])) + 1
       theta = (css[rho-1] - S) / float(rho)
    else:
       # use mean? 
       theta = (sum_v - S) / float(n)
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    out_sum = float(np.sum(out))
    if out_sum <=0: return np.ones(n)*S/n
    return out * (S/out_sum)
```
If fast path returns after scaling, sum exactly S? Multiply by S/out_sum changes entries, still nonnegative. Good.
For v with small negatives but could be fixed by subtracting negative theta? The fast path only if min>=0; else sort. Fine.

This helps significantly.

Now, `project_simplex` for QP dimension K small; fast path likely.

Need note: In fast path, if `theta <= min_v` but theta could be negative and subtract adds mass; out nonnegative. Good. If `sum_v` is very different from S, theta large; condition may fail if theta>min_v.

Now, smooth noise direction function:
```
def smooth_noise(p, rng, scale=0.1):
    n=len(p); inv_n=1.0/n
    d=np.zeros(n)
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    for k in range(1,5):
       freq = int(k) * int(rng.integers(1,4))
       phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       d += rng.normal(0,1.0)*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*t + phase)
    d -= d.mean()
    dn=float(np.linalg.norm(d))
    if dn>0: d/=dn
    return project_simplex(p + scale*inv_n*d, 1.0)
```
Scale relative RMS; if scale=0.1, RMS change 10%? d norm 1, step 0.1/n absolute; p mean 1/n, relative RMS 0.1. Good.
Use scale increasing on stuck: e.g., 0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5.

Potential issue: Smooth noise may create negative after project, okay.

```
def interp_candidates(p, endpoint, factor):
```

`normalize_prob`: if p is list, convert. If p empty? return empty? In our use nonempty. If len 0, return array.
```
def normalize_prob(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(p.size)
    return p / s
```
If p.size=0 division by zero; handle.

`project_simplex` for empty:
```
if n==0: return v
```

`fft_convolve_self`: For n=0? return empty.
```
def fft_convolve_self(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1); n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    size=2*n-1
    fsize=1
    while fsize < size: fsize <<=1
    A=fft(a,fsize)
    c=np.real(ifft(A*A))
    return c[:size]
```
Use `np.real_if_close`? Fine.

`internal_objective`: Use fft. If s<0.01? Not for internal. Return float.

`top_k_weights`: Need handle K maybe >n.
```
def top_k_weights(c, K, submode='power'):
    c = np.asarray(c,float).clip(0,None)
    n=c.size
    K=int(K)
    if K <=0: K=1
    if K >= n:
       idx=np.arange(n)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    vals=c[idx]
    vmax=float(vals.max())
    w=np.ones(idx.size)/idx.size
    if vmax > 1e-300:
       if submode == 'uniform': w=np.ones(idx.size)/idx.size
       elif submode == 'power':
          w=(vals/vmax)**4
          s=w.sum(); if s>0: w/=s else uniform
       elif submode == 'soft':
          temp=max(0.02*vmax, 1e-300)
          np.exp((vals-vmax)/temp, out=w)
          s=w.sum(); ...
       else: uniform
    return idx,w
```
For soft with vals 0, exp(-large)=0. If all vals 0 (vmax 0), uniform.

`weights_top_array`:
```
def weights_top_array(c,K,submode):
    cclipped=np.maximum(np.asarray(c,float),0)
    idx,wK=top_k_weights(cclipped,K,submode)
    wfull=np.zeros(cclipped.size,float)
    wfull[idx]=wK
    return wfull
```

`weights_full_threshold`:
```
def weights_full_threshold(c, beta_rel=10.0, frac=0.7):
    c=np.maximum(np.asarray(c,float),0)
    n=c.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    cmax=float(c.max())
    w=np.zeros(n,float)
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
       w[0]=1; return w
    mask = c >= (frac * cmax)
    cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt < 16:
       K=32 if n>=32 else n
       idx=wK=top_k_weights(c, K, 'soft')
       w[idx]=wK; return w
    z = beta_rel * (c - cmax)
    # Only mask; other set -inf
    # To avoid -inf warnings, use np.where
    z = np.where(mask, z, -745.0)  # exp(-745) ~ 5e-324
    w = np.exp(z)
    s=float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
       idx,wK=top_k_weights(c, min(64,n), 'soft')
       w=np.zeros(n); w[idx]=wK; return w
    w/=s
    return w
```
Using -745 may underflow to 5e-324, sum okay. If beta_rel small and mask all, fine. If mask count huge and beta_rel=5, weights broad.

`grad_array`: Need if w size mismatch? Ensure `w = np.asarray(w,float)`. If p size n, w size should 2n-1; if not, adjust? We'll assume. Could pad/truncate.
```
def grad_array(p,w):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).reshape(-1); n=p.size
    w=np.asarray(w,float).reshape(-1)
    if w.size < 2*n-1:
       w=np.pad(w, (0, (2*n-1)-w.size))
    elif w.size > 2*n-1:
       w=w[:2*n-1]
    L=1
    need = w.size + n - 1
    while L < need: L <<=1
    W=fft(w,L); A=fft(p,L)
    g=np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A)))[:n]
    g *= 2.0
    g = np.real(g)
    # small negatives
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g
```
Multiplying by 2 after clip? If g has negative due roundoff but true positive, clip after 2. Good.

`qp_full_weights`: Need return full w. Use `top_k_weights` for idx,wK. K choose:

```
K=int(K);
n=len(c)
K=min(K, max(4, n//1024?))? Use passed K. if K<1 K=1.
idx,wK=top_k_weights(c,K,submode)
if len(idx)<=1: wfull zeros; wfull[idx]=1; return
# build H
H=np.zeros((n_vals, len(idx)), float) where n_vals=len(p)
for j,t in enumerate(idx): ... H[...]=2.0 * p[...]
Hc = H - H.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)
A = Hc.T @ Hc
trace=float(np.trace(A))
w=wK.copy()
if trace > 1e-300:
   lr = 2.0/(trace + 1e-300)
   for _ in range(20):
      grad=2.0*(A@w)
      w = w - lr*grad
      w = project_simplex(w, 1.0)
      w = np.maximum(w,0.0); s=w.sum(); if s>0: w/=s else w=wK.copy()
   # mix
   w = 0.5*w + 0.5*wK; w/=w.sum()
wfull=np.zeros(len(c)); wfull[idx]=w; return wfull
```
But `project_simplex` imported? It's top-level. Good.
Need `p` length equals original n. H mean centering: `H.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)` returns shape (1,K). Good.

```
def default_modes(high_quality=True):

       return [

          ('top', 24, 'soft'),
          ('full', 5.0, 0.7),
          ('top', 64, 'soft'),
          ('full', 25.0, 0.8),
          ('top', 16, 'power'),
          ('full', 100.0, 0.9),
          ('top', 96, 'uniform'),
```

`linear_optimize`: Let's write carefully with variables.
```
def linear_optimize(p_in, max_time, rng, high_quality=True):
    p = normalize_prob(p_in)
    n = len(p)
    inv_n = 1/float(n)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(max_time))
    c = fft_convolve_self(p)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    score = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    obj = 2.0*float(n)*score
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score; best_obj=obj
    modes=default_modes(high_quality)
    eta=0.05
    it=0; noimp=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # if very close deadline, break
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5: break
       it += 1
       # current c should correspond to p. If p changed, we set c.
       cmax = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
       if cmax < best_score - 1e-15:
           best_score=cmax; best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=2.0*n*best_score
       # choose weights
       used_qp=False
       if high_quality and it > 80 and (noimp > 10 or it % 40 == 0):
           try:
               w = qp_full_weights(p, c, K=24, submode='soft')
               used_qp=True
           except Exception:
               w = None
       if not used_qp:
           mode = modes[it % len(modes)]
           if mode[0] == 'full':
               w = weights_full_threshold(c, mode[1], mode[2])
           else:
               w = weights_top_array(c, mode[1], mode[2])
       g = grad_array(p, w)
       gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
       if gn < 1e-300:
           # no direction, noise
           noimp += 1
           if noimp % 10 == 0:
               p = smooth_noise(best_p, rng, 0.05 + 0.05*(noimp//10))
               c = fft_convolve_self(p); c = np.maximum(c,0.0); score=float(c.max())
               if score < best_score: update
               eta=0.05
           continue
       g = g / gn
       base = eta * inv_n
       cands = []
       # factor 0.5
       q_half = project_simplex(p - 0.5*base*g, 1.0)
       cands.append((q_half, 0.5))
       # factor 1
       q_one = project_simplex(p - 1.0*base*g, 1.0)
       cands.append((q_one, 1.0))
       if eta < 5.0:
           q_two = project_simplex(p - 2.0*base*g, 1.0)
           cands.append((q_two, 2.0))
       if high_quality and eta < 2.5:
           q_four = project_simplex(p - 4.0*base*g, 1.0)
           cands.append((q_four, 4.0))
       if high_quality:
           q_quarter = p + 0.5*(q_half - p)
           q_quarter = np.maximum(q_quarter, 0.0)
           ss = float(q_quarter.sum())
           if ss > 0: q_quarter *= 1.0/ss
           else: q_quarter = p.copy()
           cands.append((q_quarter, 0.25))
       # evaluate candidates
       best_cand = None; best_cand_c = None; best_s = score + 1e-15; best_factor = 0.0
       for cand, factor in cands:
           # Optional skip if time very low? do anyway
           cc = fft_convolve_self(cand)
           cc = np.maximum(cc, 0.0)
           cs = float(cc.max()) if cc.size else 0.0
           if cs < best_s:
               best_s = cs; best_cand = cand; best_cand_c = cc; best_factor = factor
       if best_cand is not None and best_s < score - 1e-13:
           p = best_cand; c = best_cand_c; score = best_s
           noimp = 0
           if best_factor >= 4.0:
               eta = min(20.0, eta*1.8)
           elif best_factor >= 2.0:
               eta = min(10.0, eta*1.3)
           elif best_factor >= 1.0:
               eta = min(10.0, eta*1.05)
           elif best_factor < 0.5:
               eta = max(0.002, eta*0.7)
           else:
               eta = max(0.002, eta*0.9)
       else:
           noimp += 1
           if noimp <= 5:
               eta = max(0.002, eta*0.75)
           elif noimp <= 15:
               eta = min(10.0, eta*1.2)
           elif noimp > 40:
               # noise reset
               scale = 0.05 + 0.05*(noimp % 10)
               if noimp > 100: scale = 0.5
               p = smooth_noise(best_p, rng, scale)
               c = fft_convolve_self(p); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); score=float(c.max())
               if score < best_score:
                   best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=2*n*score
               noimp=0; eta=0.05
       # update best if current p somehow better? already at start.
    # final normalize
    p_best=normalize_prob(best_p)
    # compute final obj from best_p to be safe
    cbest=fft_convolve_self(p_best); cbest=np.maximum(cbest,0); bscore=float(cbest.max()) if cbest.size else 0
    bobj=2.0*float(n)*bscore
    return p_best, bobj
```
Potential issue: `best_obj` updated when best_p updated. If noise updates best_p, need update best_obj. We do in some places but not in the `cmax<best_score` at start? We update best_obj? In line: `if cmax < best_score - ...: best_score=cmax; best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=2.0*n*best_score` yes.
When no improvement but p unchanged, no update.
When candidate improves, best_p not updated unless cmax update at start next iteration. Could update immediately to not lose if time ends:
```
if best_s < best_score: best_score=best_s; best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=2*n*best_s
```
Do after p=best_cand. Good.
Also after noise reset if score < best_score. Good.

In candidate eval, if best_s is much worse than score but all candidates worse, no update. Good.

If `gn` zero often (direction zero) and noimp increments; after 10 noise. Good.

Performance: It computes c at start of loop? We don't recompute c for p at every iteration unless changed; we use current c. When candidate improves, c updated. When no improve, c stays. Good. When p is not updated, c correct. Projected candidates not best. Great. So one conv per candidate only, no extra.

In noise reset with `gn` zero, we recompute c. Good.

Potential issue: `modes[it % len(modes)]` with it increment; after noise reset it not reset, fine.

QP uses `c` current. If c from p but p sum=1. Good.

Now, if `base` very small and q projections equal p, candidates same; no improvement. noimp triggers eta increase/decrease.

Now, `smooth_noise` uses `project_simplex(p + scale*inv_n*d,1)`. If scale=0.5 and d normalized, step=0.5/n, RMS change 50%? Actually d norm=1, step absolute=0.5/n, p mean=1/n, relative RMS=0.5 (50%). Good.

Now, `default_modes` high includes full threshold. Full mode cost exp over c length and grad FFT. Good.

Now, in propose, after raw evaluation, we need store objective and p. We'll use list of tuples `(obj, L, p, name)`.

Detailed propose code:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try: budget_s=float(budget_s)
    except: budget_s=2400.0
    deadline = start + max(0.0, budget_s - 45.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # load parent
    p_parent = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size >= 32:
            p_parent = normalize_prob(arr)
    except Exception:
        p_parent = None
    if p_parent is None:
        p_parent = make_ramp_seed(8192, rng, reverse=False, alpha=3.0)
    n_parent = len(p_parent)
    # candidate lengths
    lengths=[]
    for L in [n_parent, 8192, 16384, 32768]:
        L=int(L)
        if L >= 1024 and L <= 32768:
```

Implement:
```
def trim_tiny(p, tol=1e-12):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).clip(0,None)
    n=p.size
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return p
    threshold=tol
    i=0
    while i<n and p[i] < threshold: i+=1
    j=n-1
    while j>=i and p[j] < threshold: j-=1
    if i>0 or j<n-1:
       p=p[i:j+1]
       s=p.sum(); if s>0: p=p/s
       if p.size>=2: return p
    return p
```
Use threshold 1e-12. If all trimmed, fallback uniform. Could improve. But if length becomes far smaller than expected, objective might be better or worse? Removing tiny mass shouldn't worsen much. In propose final, after global_p, compute obj; try trim, compute obj_trim; if lower, use. This is safe. It may reduce n and evaluation time. Good.

```
   w=weights_full_threshold(c, mode[1], mode[2])
else:
   w=weights_top_array(c, mode[1], mode[2])
```

```python
"""
Minimize  2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2  over non-negative sequences.

The optimizer:
- resamples the provided parent sequence to several lengths,
- uses diverse low- and high-quality active-peak gradient directions,
- uses exact projected-subset feasible line searches,
- periodically solves a small active-set quadratic direction,
- randomly smooth-restarts from the best point when stalled.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.fft import fft, ifft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import fft, ifft


def normalize_prob(p):
    """Clamp to non-negative and rescale to sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(p.size)
    return p / s


def project_simplex(v, S=1.0):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=S}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    min_v = float(np.min(v))
    sum_v = float(np.sum(v))

    # Fast path: no clipping needed.
    if min_v >= 0.0:
        theta = (sum_v - float(S)) / float(n)
        if theta <= min_v:
            out = v - theta
            out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
            s = float(out.sum())
            if s <= 0.0:
                return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * (float(S) / n)
            return out * (float(S) / s)

    # Fallback: sorted standard algorithm.
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx - (css - float(S)) > 0.0
    if np.any(cond):
        rho = int(np.max(np.nonzero(cond)[0])) + 1
        theta = (css[rho - 1] - float(S)) / float(rho)
    else:
        theta = (sum_v - float(S)) / float(n)

    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * (float(S) / n)
    return out * (float(S) / s)


def fft_convolve_self(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution of a via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = 1
    while fsize < size:
        fsize <<= 1
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))
    return c[:size]


def internal_objective(p):
    """Exact objective used internally, scale invariant."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.01:
        return np.inf
    c = fft_convolve_self(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    cmax = float(np.max(np.maximum(c, 0.0)))
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def resample_sequence(p, m):
    """Resample a non-negative probability vector to length m by CDF interpolation."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(m, 1), dtype=np.float64) / float(max(m, 1))
    s = float(p.sum())
    if p.size == 0 or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    p = p / s
    n = p.size
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    F = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    F[0] = 0.0
    F[1:] = np.cumsum(p)
    F = np.maximum.accumulate(F)
    F[-1] = 1.0
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, F)
    q = np.diff(y)
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, None)
    sq = float(q.sum())
    if sq <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    return q / sq


def make_ramp_seed(n, rng, reverse=False, alpha=None):
    """Create an exponential ramp seed normalized to sum 1."""
    n = int(n)
    if alpha is None:
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 6.0))
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False, dtype=np.float64)
    if reverse:
        arr = np.exp(float(alpha) * (1.0 - t))
    else:
        arr = np.exp(float(alpha) * t)
    return normalize_prob(arr)


def make_fourier_seed(n, rng):
    """Create a smooth random low-frequency non-negative seed."""
    n = int(n)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, 6):
        f1 = int(k) * int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        f2 = int(k) * int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        ph1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        ph2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a1 = float(rng.uniform(-0.25, 0.25)) / (0.5 * float(k))
        a2 = float(rng.uniform(-0.15, 0.15)) / float(k)
        arr += a1 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * t + ph1)
        arr += a2 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f2 * t + ph2)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 1e-3)
    return normalize_prob(arr)


def smooth_noise(p, rng, scale=0.1):
    """Add a smooth random feasible perturbation to p."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, 5):
        f = int(k) * int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        coef = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0))
        d += coef * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * t + ph)
    d -= float(d.mean())
    dn = float(np.linalg.norm(d))
    if dn > 0.0:
        d /= dn
    step = float(scale) / float(n)
    return project_simplex(p + step * d, 1.0)


def top_k_weights(c, K, submode="power"):
    """Return top K indices and normalized weights."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).clip(0.0, None)
    n = c.size
    K = int(K)
    if K <= 0:
        K = 1
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.int64), np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if K >= n:
        idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:].astype(np.int64)
    vals = c[idx]
    vmax = float(vals.max())
    m = idx.size
    w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    if vmax > 1e-300:
        if submode == "uniform":
            w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
        elif submode == "power":
            w = (vals / vmax) ** 4.0
            s = float(w.sum())
            if s > 0.0:
                w /= s
            else:
                w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
        elif submode == "soft":
            temp = max(0.02 * vmax, 1e-300)
            w = np.exp((vals - vmax) / temp)
            s = float(w.sum())
            if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
                w /= s
            else:
                w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
        else:
            w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    return idx, w


def weights_top_array(c, K, submode="power"):
    """Create a full weight array over convolution lags from a top-K rule."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).clip(0.0, None)
    idx, wK = top_k_weights(c, K, submode)
    wfull = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
    if idx.size:
        wfull[idx] = wK
    return wfull


def weights_full_threshold(c, beta_rel=10.0, frac=0.7):
    """
    Softmax weights over a near-maximum band of lags.
    beta_rel is beta*cmax, frac is the relative threshold for the band.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).clip(0.0, None)
    n = c.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        w[0] = 1.0
        return w

    mask = c >= (float(frac) * cmax)
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt < 16:
        K = min(32, n)
        idx, wK = top_k_weights(c, K, "soft")
        wfull = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        wfull[idx] = wK
        return wfull

    z = float(beta_rel) * (c - cmax)
    z = np.where(mask, z, -745.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        K = min(64, n)
        idx, wK = top_k_weights(c, K, "soft")
        wfull = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        wfull[idx] = wK
        return wfull
    w /= s
    return w


def grad_array(p, w):
    """
    Gradient of the weighted active conv terms w.r.t. p.
    w is a weight vector over the 2n-1 convolution lags, normalized to sum 1.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = p.size
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    need_len = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size < need_len:
        w = np.pad(w, (0, need_len - w.size), mode="constant")
    elif w.size > need_len:
        w = w[:need_len]

    fsize = 1
    L = w.size + n - 1
    while fsize < L:
        fsize <<= 1

    W = fft(w, fsize)
    A = fft(p, fsize)
    g = np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A)))[:n]
    g *= 2.0
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def qp_full_weights(p, c, K=24, submode="soft"):
    """
    Build a small active-set QP direction权重.
    For active lags, choose weights minimizing the projected gradient norm,
    then mix with value-based weights for robust descent.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).clip(0.0, None)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0 or c.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)

    K = int(K)
    if K <= 0:
        K = 1
    K = min(K, c.size)

    idx, w_top = top_k_weights(c, K, submode)
    K = idx.size
    if K <= 1:
        wfull = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        if K == 1:
            wfull[idx[0]] = 1.0
        return wfull

    H = np.zeros((n, K), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(K):
        t = int(idx[j])
        if t < n:
            length = t + 1
            H[0:length, j] = 2.0 * p[t::-1]
        else:
            start = t - (n - 1)
            beg = t - (n - 1)
            end = n - 1
            H[start:n, j] = 2.0 * p[beg:end + 1][::-1]

    Hc = H - H.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)
    A = Hc.T @ Hc
    trace = float(np.trace(A))

    w = np.asarray(w_top, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if trace > 1e-300:
        lr = 2.0 / (trace + 1e-300)
        for _ in range(20):
            gradw = 2.0 * (A @ w)
            w = w - lr * gradw
            w = project_simplex(w, 1.0)
            w = np.maximum(w, 0.0)
            s = float(w.sum())
            if s > 0.0:
                w /= s
            else:
                w = np.asarray(w_top, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    # Blend with value-based weights to avoid a purely stationary null direction.
    w = 0.5 * w + 0.5 * np.asarray(w_top, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.asarray(w_top, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    else:
        w /= s

    wfull = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
    wfull[idx] = w
    return wfull


def default_modes(high_quality=True):
    """Mode cycle for the local optimizer."""
    if high_quality:
        return [
            ("top", 8, "power"),
            ("top", 24, "soft"),
            ("full", 5.0, 0.7),
            ("top", 64, "soft"),
            ("full", 25.0, 0.8),
            ("top", 16, "power"),
            ("full", 100.0, 0.9),
            ("top", 96, "uniform"),
        ]
    else:
        return [
            ("top", 8, "power"),
            ("top", 24, "soft"),
            ("full", 10.0, 0.7),
            ("top", 48, "soft"),
        ]


def linear_optimize(p_in, max_time, rng, high_quality=True):
    """
    Local optimizer.
    Returns (best_probability_vector, best_objective).
    """
    p = normalize_prob(p_in)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, np.inf
    inv_n = 1.0 / float(n)

    start = time.monotonic()
    max_time = float(max(0.0, max_time))
    deadline = start + max_time

    c = fft_convolve_self(p)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    score = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_score = score
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * best_score)

    modes = default_modes(high_quality)
    eta = 0.05
    it = 0
    noimp = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5:
            break

        it += 1
        cmax = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
        if cmax < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = cmax
            best_p = p.copy()
            best_obj = float(2.0 * n * best_score)

        used_qp = False
        if high_quality and it > 80 and (noimp > 10 or it % 40 == 0):
            try:
                w = qp_full_weights(p, c, K=24, submode="soft")
                used_qp = True
            except Exception:
                w = None

        if not used_qp:
            mode = modes[it % len(modes)]
            if mode[0] == "full":
                w = weights_full_threshold(c, mode[1], mode[2])
            else:
                w = weights_top_array(c, mode[1], mode[2])

        g = grad_array(p, w)
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gn < 1e-300:
            noimp += 1
            if noimp % 10 == 0:
                scale = 0.05 + 0.05 * float((noimp // 10) % 10)
                p = smooth_noise(best_p, rng, scale)
                c = fft_convolve_self(p)
                c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
                score = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
                if score < best_score:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * best_score)
                eta = 0.05
            continue

        g = g / gn
        base = eta * inv_n

        cands = []

        q_half = project_simplex(p - 0.5 * base * g, 1.0)
        cands.append((q_half, 0.5))

        q_one = project_simplex(p - 1.0 * base * g, 1.0)
        cands.append((q_one, 1.0))

        if eta < 5.0:
            q_two = project_simplex(p - 2.0 * base * g, 1.0)
            cands.append((q_two, 2.0))

        if high_quality and eta < 2.5:
            q_four = project_simplex(p - 4.0 * base * g, 1.0)
            cands.append((q_four, 4.0))

        if high_quality:
            q_quarter = p + 0.5 * (q_half - p)
            q_quarter = np.maximum(q_quarter, 0.0)
            sq = float(q_quarter.sum())
            if sq > 0.0:
                q_quarter *= 1.0 / sq
            else:
                q_quarter = p.copy()
            cands.append((q_quarter, 0.25))

        best_cand = None
        best_cand_c = None
        best_s = score + 1e-15
        best_factor = 0.0

        for cand, factor in cands:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.25:
                break
            cc = fft_convolve_self(cand)
            cc = np.maximum(cc, 0.0)
            cs = float(cc.max()) if cc.size else 0.0
            if cs < best_s:
                best_s = cs
                best_cand = cand
                best_cand_c = cc
                best_factor = factor

        if best_cand is not None and best_s < score - 1e-13:
            p = best_cand
            c = best_cand_c
            score = best_s
            noimp = 0

            if best_s < best_score:
                best_score = best_s
                best_p = p.copy()
                best_obj = float(2.0 * n * best_score)

            if best_factor >= 4.0:
                eta = min(20.0, eta * 1.8)
            elif best_factor >= 2.0:
                eta = min(10.0, eta * 1.3)
            elif best_factor >= 1.0:
                eta = min(10.0, eta * 1.05)
            elif best_factor < 0.5:
                eta = max(0.002, eta * 0.7)
            else:
                eta = max(0.002, eta * 0.9)
        else:
            noimp += 1
            if noimp <= 5:
                eta = max(0.002, eta * 0.75)
            elif noimp <= 15:
                eta = min(10.0, eta * 1.2)
            elif noimp > 40:
                scale = 0.05 + 0.05 * float((noimp % 10))
                if noimp > 100:
                    scale = 0.5
                p = smooth_noise(best_p, rng, scale)
                c = fft_convolve_self(p)
                c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
                score = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
                if score < best_score:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * best_score)
                noimp = 0
                eta = 0.05

    best_p = normalize_prob(best_p)
    cb = fft_convolve_self(best_p)
    cb = np.maximum(cb, 0.0)
    bs = float(cb.max()) if cb.size else 0.0
    return best_p, float(2.0 * n * bs)


def trim_tiny(p, tol=1e-12):
    """Remove negligible endpoint mass, then renormalize."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    n = p.size
    if n < 2:
        return p
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return p

    i = 0
    while i < n and p[i] < float(tol):
        i += 1
    j = n - 1
    while j >= i and p[j] < float(tol):
        j -= 1

    if i > 0 or j < n - 1:
        out = p[i:j + 1]
        so = float(out.sum())
        if out.size >= 2 and so > 0.0:
            out = out / so
            return out
    return p


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Main entry point.
    Searches for a non-negative sequence minimizing the evaluator objective.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s <= 0.0:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    # Leave reserve for finalization/optional verification.
    deadline = start + max(0.0, budget_s - 45.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Load parent if available.
    p_parent = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size >= 32:
            p_parent = normalize_prob(arr)
    except Exception:
        p_parent = None

    if p_parent is None:
        p_parent = make_ramp_seed(8192, rng, reverse=False, alpha=3.0)

    n_parent = p_parent.size

    # Candidate lengths.
    lengths = []
    for L in [n_parent, 8192, 16384, 32768]:
        L = int(L)
        if 1024 <= L <= 32768:
            ok = True
            for x in lengths:
                if x == L:
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                lengths.append(L)

    for m in [2, 4, 8, 16]:
        L = int(n_parent * m)
        if 1024 <= L <= 32768:
            ok = True
            for x in lengths:
                if x == L:
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                lengths.append(L)

    if not lengths:
        lengths = [8192]

    # Keep at most four lengths: prefer standard sizes.
    if len(lengths) > 4:
        pref = []
        for L in [8192, 16384, 32768]:
            if L in lengths:
                pref.append(L)
        if len(pref) >= 1:
            lengths = pref
        if len(lengths) > 4:
            lengths = lengths[:4]

    # Raw seeds from parent resampling.
    seeds = []
    for L in lengths:
        p = resample_sequence(p_parent, L)
        c = fft_convolve_self(p)
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        cm = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
        obj = float(2.0 * L * cm)
        print("raw length %d objective %.6f" % (L, obj))
        seeds.append((obj, L, p.copy()))

    # Add a couple of independent seeds at the best raw length.
    if seeds:
        best_raw_obj = seeds[0][0]
        best_raw_L = seeds[0][1]
        best_raw_p = seeds[0][2]
        for obj, L, p in seeds:
            if obj < best_raw_obj:
                best_raw_obj = obj
                best_raw_L = L
                best_raw_p = p

        if len(seeds) < 4 and 1024 <= best_raw_L <= 32768:
            p_extra = make_ramp_seed(best_raw_L, rng, reverse=True, alpha=2.0)
            c = fft_convolve_self(p_extra)
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            obj = float(2.0 * best_raw_L * float(c.max()))
            print("extra ramp length %d objective %.6f" % (best_raw_L, obj))
            seeds.append((obj, best_raw_L, p_extra))

        if len(seeds) < 5 and 1024 <= best_raw_L <= 32768:
            p_extra = make_fourier_seed(best_raw_L, rng)
            c = fft_convolve_self(p_extra)
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            obj = float(2.0 * best_raw_L * float(c.max()))
            print("extra fourier length %d objective %.6f" % (best_raw_L, obj))
            seeds.append((obj, best_raw_L, p_extra))

    if not seeds:
        p_fallback = make_ramp_seed(8192, rng, reverse=False, alpha=2.0)
        obj_fallback = internal_objective(p_fallback)
        return [float(x) for x in p_fallback]

    global_obj = seeds[0][0]
    global_p = seeds[0][2]
    for obj, L, p in seeds:
        if obj < global_obj:
            global_obj = obj
            global_p = p.copy()

    print("initial best objective %.6f length %d" % (global_obj, len(global_p)))

    # Exploration: short optimizer runs from diverse seeds.
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now < deadline - 180.0:
        explore_total = min(240.0, max(30.0, (deadline - now) * 0.15))
        per = explore_total / float(max(1, len(seeds)))
        per = min(per, 90.0)

        # Explore the best raw seed first, then others.
        order = []
        best_idx = 0
        best_val = seeds[0][0]
        for i in range(len(seeds)):
            if seeds[i][0] < best_val:
                best_val = seeds[i][0]
                best_idx = i
        order.append(best_idx)
        for i in range(len(seeds)):
            if i != best_idx:
                order.append(i)

        for idx in order:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 180.0:
                break
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem <= 0.0:
                break
            seg = min(per, rem - 180.0)
            if seg < 5.0:
                break
            obj0, L0, p0 = seeds[idx]
            p_opt, obj_opt = linear_optimize(p0, seg, rng, high_quality=False)
            if np.isfinite(obj_opt) and obj_opt < global_obj:
                global_obj = obj_opt
                global_p = p_opt.copy()
                print("exploration improved length %d objective %.6f" % (len(global_p), global_obj))

    # Final long polish from the global best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 10.0:
        p_opt, obj_opt = linear_optimize(global_p, rem, rng, high_quality=True)
        if np.isfinite(obj_opt) and obj_opt < global_obj:
            global_obj = obj_opt
            global_p = p_opt.copy()
        print("after polish objective %.6f length %d" % (global_obj, len(global_p)))

    # Final normalization and safe fallback.
    if global_p is None or global_p.size < 2 or not np.isfinite(global_obj):
        global_p = make_ramp_seed(8192, rng, reverse=False, alpha=2.0)
        global_obj = internal_objective(global_p)

    global_p = normalize_prob(global_p)
    final_obj = internal_objective(global_p)

    # Optional safe trimming of numerically negligible endpoints.
    p_trim = trim_tiny(global_p, tol=1e-12)
    if p_trim.size >= 64:
        obj_trim = internal_objective(p_trim)
        if np.isfinite(obj_trim) and obj_trim < final_obj:
            global_p = p_trim
            final_obj = obj_trim
            global_obj = min(global_obj, obj_trim)

    # Ensure sum is safely above evaluator threshold.
    s = float(global_p.sum())
    if s <= 0.01:
        global_p = np.ones(8192, dtype=np.float64) / 8192.0
        final_obj = internal_objective(global_p)
    else:
        global_p = global_p / s

    final_list = [float(x) for x in np.clip(global_p, 0.0, 1000.0)]

    print("final internal objective %.6f length %d" % (final_obj, len(final_list)))

    # Very short verification for smaller lengths only, if budget remains.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    if len(final_list) <= 16384 and (budget_s - elapsed) > 15.0:
        try:
            ver = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
            print("evaluate_sequence final %.6f" % (ver if np.isfinite(ver) else np.inf))
        except Exception as e:
            print("verify exception: %s" % str(e))

    return final_list
```
